\displaystyle+e^{\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}t}\left(C(\frac{1}{1+\eta^{2}})(t^{3}+t^{2}+t)\frac{e^{\left(\sum_{k=1}^{3}\frac{\gamma^{k}A_{1,k}}{(1+\eta^{2})^{k}}-\frac{\gamma^{4}K_{1}}{\left(\eta^{2}+1\right)^{4}}\right)t}}{(1+\eta^{2})^{3}}\right),\quad\eta\rightarrow\infty,

where we use the Lagrangian remainder with
\gamma\in(0,1)
, and the coefficient
C(\frac{1}{1+\eta^{2}})
is a function of
\frac{1}{1+\eta^{2}}
. By direct calculation, we obtain that
C(\frac{1}{1+\eta^{2}})
is analytic and uniformly bounded. On the other hand, from Lemma
3.3
, we know
\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}<-\sigma_{0}^{*}<0
. Thus we can use the factor
e^{\left(\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}+\sigma_{0}^{*}\right)t}
to absorb
t
and
t^{2}
. Moreover, as
|\eta|
can be chosen to be sufficiently large, the factor
e^{\left(\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}+\sigma_{0}^{*}\right)t}
can also absorb the exponential part in the remainder. More precisely, we have

(3.27)

\displaystyle e^{\lambda_{1}^{*}t}-e^{\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}t}

\displaystyle=e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t}\left(\frac{A_{1,1}te^{\left(\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}+\sigma_{0}^{*}\right)t}}{(1+\eta^{2})}+\frac{\frac{A_{1,1}^{2}t^{2}e^{\left(\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}+\sigma_{0}^{*}\right)t}}{2}-A_{1,1}te^{\left(\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}+\sigma_{0}^{*}\right)t}+A_{1,2}te^{\left(\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}+\sigma_{0}^{*}\right)t}}{(1+\eta^{2})^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t}C(\frac{1}{1+\eta^{2}})(t^{3}+t^{2}+t)\frac{e^{\left(\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}+\sigma_{0}^{*}\right)t+\left(\sum_{k=1}^{3}\frac{\gamma^{k}A_{1,k}}{(1+\eta^{2})^{k}}-\frac{\gamma^{4}K_{1}}{\left(\eta^{2}+1\right)^{4}}\right)t}}{(1+\eta^{2})^{3}}

\displaystyle=O(1)te^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t}\left(\frac{1}{(1+\eta^{2})}+\frac{1}{(1+\eta^{2})^{2}}+\frac{1}{(1+\eta^{2})^{3}}\right),\quad\eta\rightarrow\infty.

From above analysis,
\frac{1}{t}\left(e^{\lambda_{1}^{*}t}-e^{\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}t}\right)e^{\sigma_{0}^{*}t}
is analytic in the region
|Im(\eta)|<\sigma_{0}
and, according to (
3.27
), satisfies the requirement on asymptotic property in Lemma
3.5
. Therefore, we apply Lemma
3.5
to conclude that

\Lambda_{1}^{*}(x,t)=e^{\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}t}\delta(x)+O(1)te^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|}.

When
t\geq 1
, we can further use the exponential term to absorb
t
in (
3.27
). Therefore, when
t\geq 1
, we actually have

\Lambda_{1}^{*}(x,t)=e^{\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}t}\delta(x)+O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|},\quad t\geq 1.

Then, the higher oder estimates of
\Lambda_{1}^{*}(x,t)
can be obtained via similar arguments, and we omit the details.

\bullet
(Step 2:
\Lambda_{2}^{*}
and
\Lambda_{3}^{*}
) For simplicity, we only do estimates on
\Lambda_{3}^{*}
, and the same argument can be applied to
\Lambda_{2}^{*}
to obtain the desired results. According to (
3.18
), we have

e^{\lambda_{3}^{*}t}=e^{-\frac{\eta^{2}\kappa\theta_{e}}{v}t+\frac{pvp_{e}}{\kappa\theta_{e}-\mu}t+\left(\sum_{k=1}^{3}\frac{A_{3,k}}{(1+\eta^{2})^{k}}-\frac{K_{3}}{\left(\eta^{2}+1\right)^{4}}\right)t}.

\diamond
(Case 1) We first consider the case
t\geq 1
. According to Lemma
3.3
, the real part of
\lambda_{3}^{*}
has a negative upper bound. Actually, we have

(3.28)

e^{\lambda_{3}^{*}t}e^{\sigma_{0}^{*}t}=e^{\left(\lambda_{3}^{*}+\frac{\kappa\theta_{e}}{2v}(\eta^{2}+1)\right)t+\sigma_{0}^{*}t}e^{-\frac{\kappa\theta_{e}}{2v}(\eta^{2}+1)t}=O(1)e^{-\frac{\kappa\theta_{e}}{2v}(\eta^{2}+1)t}.

It is obvious that
e^{\lambda_{3}^{*}t}e^{\sigma_{0}^{*}t}
is analytic in the region
|Im(\eta)|<\sigma_{0}
. On the other hand, as
t\geq 1
, the term
e^{-\frac{\kappa\theta_{e}}{2v}(\eta^{2}+1)t}
is analytic, uniformly bounded and has the following estimate for any positive integer
m
when
\eta
tends to infinity,

(3.29)

e^{-\frac{\kappa\theta_{e}}{2v}(\eta^{2}+1)t}=O(1)\frac{1}{(1+\eta^{2})^{m}},\quad t\geq 1,\quad m\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}.

Therefore, we combine (
3.28
), (
3.29
) and apply Lemma
3.5
to obtain that

e^{\lambda_{3}^{*}t}=O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|}.

\diamond
(Case 2) Then, we consider the case
t<1
. Similar as in estimates of
\Lambda_{1}^{*}
, we know
e^{\lambda_{3}^{*}t}
is analytic in the region
|Im(\eta)|<\sigma_{0}
around the real axis. Moreover, we can view
e^{\lambda_{3}^{*}t}
as a function of
(1+\eta^{2})^{-1}
and expand
e^{\lambda_{3}^{*}t}
at
\eta=\infty
to obtain

(3.30)

\displaystyle e^{\lambda_{3}^{*}t}

\displaystyle=e^{-\frac{\eta^{2}\kappa\theta_{e}}{v}t+\frac{pvp_{e}}{\kappa\theta_{e}-\mu}t}e^{\left(\sum_{k=1}^{3}\frac{A_{3,k}}{(1+\eta^{2})^{k}}-\frac{K_{3}}{\left(\eta^{2}+1\right)^{4}}\right)t}

\displaystyle=e^{-\frac{\eta^{2}\kappa\theta_{e}}{v}t+\frac{pvp_{e}}{\kappa\theta_{e}-\mu}t}\left(1+\frac{A_{3,1}t}{(1+\eta^{2})}+\frac{\frac{A_{3,1}^{2}t^{2}}{2}-A_{3,1}t+A_{3,2}t}{(1+\eta^{2})^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+e^{-\frac{\eta^{2}\kappa\theta_{e}}{v}t+\frac{pvp_{e}}{\kappa\theta_{e}-\mu}t}\left(C(\frac{1}{1+\eta^{2}})(t^{3}+t^{2}+t)\frac{e^{\left(\sum_{k=1}^{3}\frac{\gamma^{k}A_{3,k}}{(1+\eta^{2})^{k}}-\frac{\gamma^{4}K_{3}}{\left(\eta^{2}+1\right)^{4}}\right)t}}{(1+\eta^{2})^{3}}\right),

where
\gamma
is the constant chosen in the Lagrangian remainder as before. The leading term of (
3.30
) is a heat kernel with an exponential coefficient
e^{\frac{pvp_{e}}{\kappa\theta_{e}-\mu}t}
. As
t<1
, we know the exponential coefficient is uniformly bounded, therefore we can apply the same argument as (
3.27
) to conclude that

e^{\lambda_{3}^{*}t}-e^{-\frac{\eta^{2}\kappa\theta_{e}}{v}t+\frac{pvp_{e}}{\kappa\theta_{e}-\mu}t}=O(1)\frac{t}{1+\eta^{2}}.

Then we apply Lemma
3.5
to have the zeroth order estimate of
\Lambda_{3}^{*}
as follows,

\Lambda_{3}^{*}=\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{3}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{3}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{3}t}}+O(1)te^{-\sigma_{0}|x|}=\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{3}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{3}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{3}t}}+O(1)te^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|},\quad 0<t<1,

where the last equality is due to the fact
t<1
. The first order derivative
\partial_{x}\Lambda_{3}^{*}
can be obtained via similar argument. For second order derivative
\partial^{2}_{x}\Lambda_{3}^{*}
, the estimate would be a little bit different since the appearance of a new heat kernel. More precisely, we can apply similar argument as above to have

\eta^{2}e^{\lambda_{3}^{*}t}-\eta^{2}e^{-\frac{\eta^{2}\kappa\theta_{e}}{v}t+\frac{pvp_{e}}{\kappa\theta_{e}-\mu}t}-A_{3,1}te^{-\frac{\eta^{2}\kappa\theta_{e}}{v}t+\frac{pvp_{e}}{\kappa\theta_{e}-\mu}t}=O(1)\frac{t}{1+\eta^{2}}.

Then we can apply Lemma
3.5
and use the fact
t<1
again to obtain that

\displaystyle\partial^{2}_{x}\Lambda_{3}^{*}

\displaystyle=\partial^{2}_{x}\left[\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{3}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{3}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{3}t}}\right]+A_{3,1}\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{3}t}\sqrt{t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{3}}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{3}t}}+O(1)te^{-\sigma_{0}|x|}

\displaystyle=\partial^{2}_{x}\left[\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{3}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{3}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{3}t}}\right]+tA_{3,1}\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{3}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{3}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{3}t}}+O(1)te^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|},\quad 0<t<1.

Finally, the estimate of the third order derivative
\partial^{3}_{x}\Lambda_{3}^{*}
can be obtained similarly as
\partial^{2}_{x}\Lambda_{3}^{*}
.
∎

3.2.4.
Pointwise estimate of
\mathbb{G}^{*}(x,t;\bar{U})

We already define the Fourier transform of the singular part
\mathbb{G}^{*}(x,t;\bar{U})
in (
3.22
). In previous discussion, we have obtained the point-wise estimates of the inverse Fourier transform of each component in
\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{*}(\eta,t;\bar{U})
, and now we will combine these estimates together to construct the estimates of
\mathbb{G}^{*}(x,t;\bar{U})
.

Theorem 3.1
.

Let
\star_{x}
denotes the convolution with respect to
x
. The singular part of the Green’s function defined in (
3.22
) has three parts. For the first part
\mathbb{G}^{*,1}(x,t)
, we have the following pointwise estimates,

(3.31)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mathbb{G}^{*,1}(x,t)=e^{\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}t}\delta(x)M_{1}^{*,0}+O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|},\\
&\partial_{x}\mathbb{G}^{*,1}(x,t)=e^{\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}t}\left(\frac{d}{dx}\delta(x)M_{1}^{*,0}-\delta(x)M_{1}^{*,1}\right)+O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|},\\
&\partial^{2}_{x}\mathbb{G}^{*,1}(x,t)=e^{\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}t}\left(\frac{d^{2}}{dx^{2}}\delta(x)M_{1}^{*,0}-\frac{d}{dx}\delta(x)M_{1}^{*,1}+\delta(x)\left(M_{1}^{*,2}-A_{1,1}tM_{1}^{*,0}\right)\right)+O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|}.\end{aligned}\right.

For the other two parts
\mathbb{G}^{*,2}(x,t)
and
\mathbb{G}^{*,3}(x,t)
, when
t\geq 1
, we have the estimate below,

(3.32)

\partial_{x}^{k}\mathbb{G}^{*,j}(x,t)=O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|},\quad k=0,1,2,\quad j=2,3,\quad t\geq 1.

While when
0<t<1
, we have the following estimates for
\mathbb{G}^{*,2}(x,t)
and
\mathbb{G}^{*,3}(x,t)
,

(3.33)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mathbb{G}^{*,j}(x,t)&&=&&O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|}+\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{j}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}M_{j}^{*,0},\\
&\partial_{x}\mathbb{G}^{*,j}(x,t)&&=&&O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|}+\partial_{x}\left[\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{j}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}\right]M_{j}^{*,0}-\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{j}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}M_{j}^{*,1},\\
&\partial^{2}_{x}\mathbb{G}^{*,j}(x,t)&&=&&O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|}+\partial^{2}_{x}\left[\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{j}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}\right]M_{j}^{*,0}-\partial_{x}\left[\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{j}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}\right]M_{j}^{*,1}\\
&&&&&+\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{j}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}M_{j}^{*,2},\\
&\partial^{3}_{x}\mathbb{G}^{*,j}(x,t)&&=&&O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|}+\partial^{3}_{x}\left[\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{j}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}\right]M_{j}^{*,0}-\partial^{2}_{x}\left[\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{j}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}\right]M_{j}^{*,1}\\
&&&&&+\partial_{x}\left[\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{j}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}\right]M_{j}^{*,2}+\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{j}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}M_{j}^{*,3}\end{aligned}\right.

Proof.

We will prove the estimates in two steps.

\bullet
(Step 1:
\mathbb{G}^{*,1}(x,t)
) For the part
\mathbb{G}^{*,1}(x,t)
, we first apply Lemma
3.6
and Lemma
3.7
to provide the zero order estimate as follows,

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}^{*,1}(x,t)=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left[e^{\lambda_{1}^{*}t}\hat{\mathbb{M}}_{1}^{*}\right]=\Lambda_{1}^{*}(x,t)\star_{x}\mathbb{M}_{1}^{*}(x)

\displaystyle=\left(e^{\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}t}\delta(x)+O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|}\right)\star_{x}\left(\delta(x)M_{1}^{*,0}+O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}|x|}\right)

\displaystyle=e^{\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}t}\delta(x)M_{1}^{*,0}+O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|},

Then for the first order estimate, we apply Lemma
3.6
and Lemma
3.7
again to have

\displaystyle\partial_{x}\mathbb{G}^{*,1}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\partial_{x}\Lambda_{1}^{*}(x,t)\star_{x}\mathbb{M}_{1}^{*}(x)

\displaystyle=\left(e^{\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}t}\frac{d}{dx}\delta(x)+O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|}\right)\star_{x}\mathbb{M}_{1}^{*}(x)

\displaystyle=e^{\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}t}\frac{d}{dx}\mathbb{M}_{1}^{*}(x)+O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|}

\displaystyle=e^{\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}t}\left(\frac{d}{dx}\delta(x)M_{1}^{*,0}-\delta(x)M_{1}^{*,1}+O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}|x|}\right)+O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|}

\displaystyle=e^{\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}t}\left(\frac{d}{dx}\delta(x)M_{1}^{*,0}-\delta(x)M_{1}^{*,1}\right)+O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|}.

The second order estimate can be similarly obtained.

\bullet
(Step 2:
\mathbb{G}^{*,2}(x,t)
and
\mathbb{G}^{*,3}(x,t)
) For the other two terms, we will construct the estimates in time intervals
t\geq 1
and
0<t<1
respectively. When
t\geq 1
, according to Lemma
3.6
and Lemma
3.7
, the following estimate holds for
k=0,1,2
and
j=2,3
,

\displaystyle\partial^{k}_{x}\mathbb{G}^{*,j}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\partial^{k}_{x}\Lambda_{j}^{*}(x,t)\star_{x}\mathbb{M}_{j}^{*}(x)

\displaystyle=\left(O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|}\right)\star_{x}\left(\delta(x)M_{j}^{*,0}+O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}|x|}\right)

\displaystyle=O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|}.

Then, when
0<t<1
, for the zeroth order estimates, we apply Lemma
3.6
and Lemma
3.7
to obtain for
j=2,3
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}^{*,j}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{j}^{*}(x,t)\star_{x}\mathbb{M}_{j}^{*}(x)

\displaystyle=\left(O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|}+\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{j}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}\right)\star_{x}\left(\delta(x)M_{j}^{*,0}+O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}|x|}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{j}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}M_{j}^{*,0}+O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|}.

Similarly, when
0<t<1
and
j=2,3
, we have the first order estimates as follows,

\displaystyle\partial_{x}\mathbb{G}^{*,j}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\partial_{x}\Lambda_{j}^{*}(x,t)\star_{x}\mathbb{M}_{j}^{*}(x)

\displaystyle=\left(O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|}+\partial_{x}\left[\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{j}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}\right]\right)\star_{x}\mathbb{M}_{j}^{*}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{j}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}\star_{x}\left(\frac{d}{dx}\mathbb{M}_{j}^{*}\right)+O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|}

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{j}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}\star_{x}\left(\frac{d}{dx}\delta(x)M_{j}^{*,0}-\delta(x)M_{j}^{*,1}+O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}|x|}\right)+O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|}

\displaystyle=\partial_{x}\left[\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{j}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}\right]M_{j}^{*,0}-\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{j}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}M_{j}^{*,1}+O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|}.

The higher order estimate can be obtained in a similar way, thus we omit the details and finish the proof.

∎

Then, we will estimate the time derivative of the singular part of Green’s function. As there is a diffusion term in the linearized equation (
1.14
), the first order time derivative will generate a second order space derivative. The details will be shown in the following lemma.

Theorem 3.2
.

Let
j=2,3
, the time derivatives and the mixed derivatives of the singular part have the following estimates,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbb{G}^{*,1}(x,t)=\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}e^{\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}t}\delta(x)M_{1}^{*,0}+O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|},

\displaystyle\partial_{xt}\mathbb{G}^{*,1}(x,t)=\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}e^{\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}t}\frac{d}{dx}\delta(x)M_{1}^{*,0}-\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}e^{\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}t}\delta(x)M_{1}^{*,1}+O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|},

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbb{G}^{*,j}(x,t)=O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|}+\left\{\begin{aligned} &\alpha^{*}_{j}\partial^{2}_{x}\left[\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{j}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}\right]M_{j}^{*,0}-\alpha^{*}_{j}\partial_{x}\left[\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{j}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}\right]M_{j}^{*,1}\\
&+\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{j}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}\left(\alpha^{*}_{j}M_{j}^{*,2}+\beta^{*}_{j}M_{j}^{*,0}\right),&\quad 0<t<1,\\
&0,&\quad t\geq 1,\end{aligned}\right.

\displaystyle\partial_{xt}\mathbb{G}^{*,j}(x,t)=O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|}+\left\{\begin{aligned} &\alpha^{*}_{j}\partial^{3}_{x}\left[\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{j}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}\right]M_{j}^{*,0}-\alpha^{*}_{j}\partial^{2}_{x}\left[\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{j}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}\right]M_{j}^{*,1}\\
&+\partial_{x}\left[\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{j}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}\right]\left(\alpha^{*}_{j}M_{j}^{*,2}+\beta^{*}_{j}M_{j}^{*,0}\right)\\
&+\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{j}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}\left(\alpha^{*}_{j}M_{j}^{*,3}-\beta^{*}_{j}M_{j}^{*,1}\right),&\quad 0<t<1,\\
&0,&\quad t\geq 1.\end{aligned}\right.

Proof.

For time derivative, we will provide the proof for
\partial_{t}\mathbb{G}^{*,1}
; while for mixed derivative, we will give the proof for
\partial_{xt}\mathbb{G}^{*,j}
with
j=2,3
. The other estimates can be constructed similarly. First, for
\partial_{t}\mathbb{G}^{*,1}
, the time derivative will be preserved under Fourier transform, and thus we have the following formula,

\partial_{t}\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{*,1}(\eta,t)=\lambda_{1}^{*}e^{\lambda_{1}^{*}t}\hat{\mathbb{M}}_{1}^{*}=\left(\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\frac{A_{1,k}}{(1+\eta^{2})^{k}}-\frac{K_{1}}{\left(\eta^{2}+1\right)^{4}}\right)e^{\lambda_{1}^{*}t}\hat{\mathbb{M}}_{1}^{*}.

Then we apply Lemma
3.5
and the structure of singular part in Theorem
3.1
to obtain,

(3.34)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbb{G}^{*,1}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left[\left(\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\frac{A_{1,k}}{(1+\eta^{2})^{k}}-\frac{K_{1}}{\left(\eta^{2}+1\right)^{4}}\right)e^{\lambda_{1}^{*}t}\hat{\mathbb{M}}_{1}^{*}\right]

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}\delta(x)+O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|}\right)\star_{x}\left(e^{\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}t}\delta(x)M_{1}^{*,0}+O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}e^{\frac{vp_{v}}{\mu}t}\delta(x)M_{1}^{*,0}+O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|}.

Next, for
\partial_{xt}\mathbb{G}^{*,j}
with
j=2,3
, we apply similar method as before to obtain the following exact formula under Fourier transform,

\mathcal{F}\left[\partial_{xt}\mathbb{G}^{*,j}(x,t)\right]=i\eta\lambda_{j}^{*}e^{\lambda_{j}^{*}t}\hat{\mathbb{M}}_{j}^{*}=\left(-i\alpha^{*}_{j}\eta^{3}+i\beta^{*}_{j}\eta+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\frac{A_{j,k}i\eta}{(1+\eta^{2})^{k}}-\frac{K_{j}i\eta}{\left(\eta^{2}+1\right)^{4}}\right)e^{\lambda_{j}^{*}t}\hat{\mathbb{M}}_{j}^{*}.

Then we apply inverse Fourier transform, use Lemma
3.5
and Theorem
3.1
to obtain the following estimates,

\displaystyle\partial_{xt}\mathbb{G}^{*,j}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\alpha^{*}_{j}\partial^{3}_{x}\mathbb{G}^{*,j}+\beta^{*}_{j}\partial_{x}\mathbb{G}^{*,j}+O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|}

\displaystyle=O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|}+\left\{\begin{aligned} &\alpha^{*}_{j}\partial^{3}_{x}\left[\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{j}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}\right]M_{j}^{*,0}-\alpha^{*}_{j}\partial^{2}_{x}\left[\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{j}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}\right]M_{j}^{*,1}\\
&+\partial_{x}\left[\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{j}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}\right]\left(\alpha^{*}_{j}M_{j}^{*,2}+\beta^{*}_{j}M_{j}^{*,0}\right)\\
&+\frac{e^{\beta^{*}_{j}t}}{\sqrt{4\pi\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha^{*}_{j}t}}\left(\alpha^{*}_{j}M_{j}^{*,3}-\beta^{*}_{j}M_{j}^{*,1}\right),&\quad 0<t<1,\\
&0,&\quad t\geq 1.\end{aligned}\right.

∎

3.3.
Regular parts of Green’s function

We next study the point-wise estimate of the regular parts, we define the regular part
\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}
to be the difference between the full Green’s function
\mathbb{G}
and the singular part
\mathbb{G}^{*}
, i.e.,

\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}=\mathbb{G}-\mathbb{G}^{*}.

According to (
3.9
) and (
3.22
), the Fourier transform of the regular part
\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}
has a natural representation as follows,

(3.35)

\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\dagger}(\eta,t;\bar{U})=\sum_{j=1}^{3}e^{\lambda_{j}t}\hat{\mathbb{M}}_{j}-\sum_{j=1}^{3}e^{\lambda^{*}_{j}t}\hat{\mathbb{M}}^{*}_{j}.

From (
3.35
) and Remark
3.1
, we immediately see the vanishment of
\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}
at initial time, i.e.,
\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}(x,0)=\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\dagger}(\eta,0)=0
. However, it is not convenient to invert (
3.35
) to construct the point-wise estimate of
\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}
since we do not have the full information of
\lambda_{j}
and
\mathbb{M}_{j}
. Instead, we have the linear differential equations in (
3.1
), which provides the equation of
\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}
as below,

(3.36)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{t}+F^{\prime}(\bar{U})\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{x}-B(\bar{U})\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{xx}=-\left(\mathbb{G}^{*}_{t}+F^{\prime}(\bar{U})\mathbb{G}^{*}_{x}-B(\bar{U})\mathbb{G}^{*}_{xx}\right)=:-\Psi(x,t),\\
&\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}(x,0)=0,\quad\Psi(x,t)=\sum_{j=1}^{3}\Psi_{j}(x,t),\\
&\Psi_{j}(x,t)=\mathbb{G}^{*,j}_{t}+F^{\prime}(\bar{U})\mathbb{G}^{*,j}_{x}-B(\bar{U})\mathbb{G}^{*,j}_{xx}.\end{aligned}\right.

We will see in the next lemma that, the truncation error
\Psi_{j}
owns high order regularity and fast space-time decay structure.

Lemma 3.8
.

Let
\sigma_{0}
and
\sigma_{0}^{*}
be positive constants defined in Lemma
3.3
. Then the Fourier transform
\hat{\Psi}
is analytic in the region
|Im(\eta)|<\sigma_{0}
for
j=1,2,3
. Moreover, when
\eta
tends to infinity,
\hat{\Psi}
has the following asymptotic property,

|\hat{\Psi}(\eta,t)|=O(1)e^{-\sigma^{*}_{0}t}(1+|\eta|)^{-6},\quad|\eta|\rightarrow+\infty,\quad|Im(\eta)|<\sigma_{0}.

Consequently, let
\|\cdot\|_{H^{4}(\mathbb{R})}
be the fourth order Sobolev norm, then we have

\|\Psi(x,t)\|_{H^{4}(\mathbb{R})}=O(1)e^{-\sigma^{*}_{0}t},\quad\sum_{k=0}^{4}|\partial_{x}^{k}\Psi(x,t)|=O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|}.

Proof.

We start with the Fourier transform
\hat{\Psi}_{j}
. According to (
3.22
) and (
3.36
),
\hat{\Psi}_{j}
satisfies the following equation,

(3.37)

\hat{\Psi}_{j}(\eta,t)=\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{*,j}_{t}+i\eta F^{\prime}(\bar{U})\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{*,j}+\eta^{2}B(\bar{U})\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{*,j}=\left(\lambda_{j}^{*}+i\eta F^{\prime}(\bar{U})+\eta^{2}B(\bar{U})\right)e^{\lambda_{j}^{*}t}\hat{\mathbb{M}}_{j}^{*}.

From Lemma
3.3
, we know
\lambda_{j}^{*}
are analytic in the region
|Im(\eta)|<\sigma_{0}
for
j=1,2,3
. Then the above formula immediately implies that
\hat{\Psi}_{j}
, and thus
\hat{\Psi}
, are analytic in the region
|Im(\eta)|<\sigma_{0}
. Moreover, if we further have
|\eta|<C
for some positive constant
C
, then we can use the spectral gap of
\lambda_{j}^{*}
in Lemma
3.3
to obtain that

(3.38)

|\hat{\Psi}(\eta,t)|=O(1)e^{-\sigma^{*}_{0}t}.

Next, we will show the estimates of
\hat{\Psi}(\eta,t)
when
\eta
tends to infinity. In fact, from the equation of full Green’s function
\mathbb{G}
in (
3.2
) and the representation of
\mathbb{G}
in terms of
\mathbb{G}_{j}
in (
3.9
), we have

(3.39)

0=\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left(\lambda_{j}+i\eta F^{\prime}(\bar{U})+\eta^{2}B(\bar{U})\right)e^{\lambda_{j}t}\hat{\mathbb{M}}_{j}.

Combining (
3.37
) and (
3.39
), we obtain that

(3.40)

\displaystyle\hat{\Psi}(\eta,t)=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left(\left(\lambda_{j}^{*}+i\eta F^{\prime}(\bar{U})+\eta^{2}B(\bar{U})\right)e^{\lambda_{j}^{*}t}\hat{\mathbb{M}}_{j}^{*}-\left(\lambda_{j}+i\eta F^{\prime}(\bar{U})+\eta^{2}B(\bar{U})\right)e^{\lambda_{j}t}\hat{\mathbb{M}}_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left(\lambda_{j}^{*}-\lambda_{j}\right)e^{\lambda_{j}^{*}t}\hat{\mathbb{M}}_{j}^{*}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left(\lambda_{j}+i\eta F^{\prime}(\bar{U})+\eta^{2}B(\bar{U})\right)e^{\lambda^{*}_{j}t}\left(1-e^{(\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{j}^{*})t}\right)\hat{\mathbb{M}}^{*}_{j}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left(\lambda_{j}+i\eta F^{\prime}(\bar{U})+\eta^{2}B(\bar{U})\right)e^{\lambda_{j}t}\left(\hat{\mathbb{M}}^{*}_{j}-\hat{\mathbb{M}}_{j}\right).

According to (
3.19
) in Lemma
3.3
, we know the approximation from
\lambda_{j}^{*}
to
\lambda_{j}
is accurate up to eighth order, therefore we have the following asymptotic estimates when
\eta\rightarrow\infty
in the region
|Im(\eta)|<\sigma_{0}
,

(3.41)

\lambda_{j}^{*}=\lambda_{j}+O(1)\frac{1}{|\eta|^{8}},\quad e^{\lambda_{j}^{*}t}=e^{\lambda_{j}t}+O(1)\frac{1}{|\eta|^{8}},\quad\hat{\mathbb{M}}_{j}^{*}=\hat{\mathbb{M}}_{j}+O(1)\frac{1}{|\eta|^{8}},\quad Re(\lambda_{j})<-\sigma^{*}_{0}.

Note in the matrix
\left(\lambda_{j}+i\eta F^{\prime}(\bar{U})+\eta^{2}B(\bar{U})\right)
, the highest order of
\eta
is two. Then we combine (
3.38
), (
3.40
) and (
3.41
) to obtain that

\displaystyle|\hat{\Psi}(\eta,t)|=O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t},\qquad\quad|\eta|\leq C,\quad|Im(\eta)|<\sigma_{0},

\displaystyle|\hat{\Psi}(\eta,t)|=O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t}\frac{1}{|\eta|^{6}},\quad|\eta|\rightarrow+\infty,\quad|Im(\eta)|<\sigma_{0}.

Finally, we combine above estimates with Lemma
3.5
to conclude the estimates in space time domain,

\|\Psi(x,t)\|_{H^{4}(\mathbb{R})}=O(1)e^{-\sigma^{*}_{0}t},\quad\sum_{k=0}^{4}|\partial_{x}^{k}\Psi(x,t)|=O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|}.

∎

According to Lemma
3.8
, the source term in (
3.36
) is regular, moreover the initial data is zero in (
3.36
), therefore there is no singularity in the regular part
\mathbb{G}^{{\dagger}}
. In the following, we will split the estimates of
\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}
into two parts. We first use energy estimate to treat
\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}
in initial layer and the region outside wave cone, then we use the spectral information, the long wave and short wave decomposition to construct
\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}
inside the finite Mach region.

3.3.1.
Weighted energy estimates

In this part, we will introduce weighted energy estimate, which will give us the structure of
\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}(x,t;\bar{U})
in the region
0<t<1
and
|x|>2Mt
for some positive constants
M
related to the Mach number. First of all, in order to make sure the matrix
B
in (
1.14
) is positive semi-definite, we need the following small perturbation assumption,

(3.42)

\bar{U}=\left(\bar{v},\bar{u},\bar{E}\right),\quad\|\bar{U}-(1,0,1)\|<\varepsilon,\quad 0<\varepsilon\ll 1.

Lemma 3.9
.

Under the assumption (
3.42
), there exists a positive constant
\sigma
such that the following estimates hold when
0<t<1
,

\|e^{\sigma|x|}\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}(x,t)\|_{H^{4}(\mathbb{R})}\leq O(1)t,\quad\sum_{k=0}^{3}|\partial_{x}^{k}\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}(x,t)|\leq O(1)te^{-\sigma|x|}.

Proof.

According to constitutive relation (
1.2
) and the assumption (
3.42
), we immediately obtain that
p-p_{e}u^{2}>0
at the fixed state
\bar{U}
. Therefore, we have the following matrix
Q
is positive definite,

(3.43)

Q:=\begin{pmatrix}-p_{v}p_{e}^{-1}\left(p-p_{e}u^{2}\right)&0&0\\
0&p_{e}^{-1}\left(p-p_{e}u^{2}\right)&0\\
0&0&1\end{pmatrix}.

Then we multiply
Q
to (
3.36
) to obtain an equivalent equation as follows,

(3.44)

Q\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{t}+QF^{\prime}(\bar{U})\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{x}-QB(\bar{U})\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{xx}=-Q\Psi(x,t),

where the coefficients have the following decompositions,

\displaystyle QF^{\prime}(\bar{U})=S_{1}+P_{1},\quad S_{1}=\begin{pmatrix}0&-p_{v}p_{e}^{-1}\left(p-p_{e}u^{2}\right)&0\\
-p_{v}p_{e}^{-1}\left(p-p_{e}u^{2}\right)&-u\left(p-p_{e}u^{2}\right)&p-p_{e}u^{2}\\
0&p-p_{e}u^{2}&p_{e}u\end{pmatrix},\quad P_{1}=\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&0&0\\
p_{v}u&0&0\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle QB(\bar{U})=S_{2}+P_{2},,\quad S_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&\frac{\mu\left(p-p_{e}u^{2}\right)}{vp_{e}}&0\\
0&0&\frac{\kappa\theta_{e}}{v}\end{pmatrix},\quad P_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&0&0\\
0&\left(\frac{\mu}{v}-\frac{\kappa\theta_{e}}{v}\right)u&0\end{pmatrix},

where all the state variables take values at
\bar{U}
in (
3.44
). In (
3.44
),
S_{1}
and
P_{1}
(
S_{2}
and
P_{2}
) are the symmetric part and non-symmetric part of the matrix
QF^{\prime}(\bar{U})
(matrix
QB(\bar{U})
) respectively. Then we choose a positive constant
\sigma
such that
0<\sigma<\sigma_{0}
(given in Lemma
3.3
), multiply
e^{2\sigma x}(\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}
to (
3.44
), take the trace norm and integrate with respect to
x
to have

(3.45)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}tr\left(e^{2\sigma x}(\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}Q\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{t}\right)dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}}tr\left(e^{2\sigma x}(\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}(S_{1}+P_{1})\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{x}\right)dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}}tr\left(e^{2\sigma x}(\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}(S_{2}+P_{2})\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{xx}\right)dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}tr\left(-e^{2\sigma x}(\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}Q\Psi(x,t)\right)dx,

where
Q^{\mathsf{T}}
is the transpose of
Q
and
tr(Q)
is the trace of the matrix
Q
. Now as
S_{1}
is symmetric with all elements being constants, we have the following relation

tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}S_{1}\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{x}\right)=\frac{1}{2}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}S_{1}\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)_{x},

and similar identity holds for
S_{2}
. Therefore, we substitute above relation into (
3.45
) and apply integration by parts to conclude that

(3.46)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\sigma x}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}Q\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)dx

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sigma e^{2\sigma x}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}(S_{1})\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\sigma x}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}(P_{1})\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}}2\sigma e^{2\sigma x}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}(P_{1})\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)dx

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\sigma x}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}B(\bar{U})\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{x}\right)dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}}2\sigma^{2}e^{2\sigma x}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}(S_{2})\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}}2\sigma e^{2\sigma x}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}(P_{2})\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{x}\right)dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}tr\left(-e^{2\sigma x}(\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}Q\Psi(x,t)\right)dx.

It is obvious that
Q
is positive definite according to (
3.43
), thus the trace of
(\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}Q\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{t}
is equivalent to the trace of
(\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{t}
. Actually, we can find two positive constants
C_{1}
,
C_{2}
,
C_{3}
and apply Young’s inequality and Cauchy inequality to obtain that

(3.47)

\displaystyle 0<C_{1}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}Q\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)\leq tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)\leq C_{2}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}Q\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right),

\displaystyle 0<C_{1}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}B(\bar{U})\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{x}\right)\leq tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&1\end{pmatrix}\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{x}\right)\leq C_{2}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}B(\bar{U})\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{x}\right),

\displaystyle\left|tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}(S_{1})\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)\right|+\left|tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}(P_{1})\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)\right|+\left|tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}(S_{2})\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)\right|\leq C_{2}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right),

\displaystyle\left|tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}(P_{1})\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)\right|+\left|tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}(P_{2})\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{x}\right)\right|\leq\frac{C_{2}}{C_{3}}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&1\end{pmatrix}\mathbb{G}_{x}^{\dagger}\right)+C_{3}C_{2}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right),

\displaystyle\left|tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}Q\Psi(x,t)\right)\right|\leq C_{2}\sqrt{tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)}\sqrt{tr\left((\Psi(x,t))^{\mathsf{T}}\Psi(x,t)\right)},

where the second inequality holds because
B(\bar{U})
is positive semi-definite and the first row and first column vanish in
B(\bar{U})
. Similarly, in the fourth inequality, the first row of matrix
P_{1}
vanishes, which implies that
tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}(P_{1})\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)
does not contain the differentiation of the elements in the first row of
\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}
, and thus the fourth inequality holds. Now we combine (
3.46
), (
3.47
) and Hölder’s inequality to obtain that

(3.48)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\sigma x}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}Q\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)dx

\displaystyle\leq\left(3\sigma C_{2}^{2}+2C_{2}^{2}\sigma^{2}+\left(1+2\sigma\right)C_{3}C_{2}^{2}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\sigma x}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}Q\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)dx

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{C_{2}}{C_{3}}\left(1+2\sigma\right)-\frac{1}{C_{2}}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\sigma x}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&1\end{pmatrix}\mathbb{G}_{x}^{\dagger}\right)dx

\displaystyle+(C_{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\sigma x}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}Q\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)dx}\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\sigma x}tr\left((\Psi(x,t))^{\mathsf{T}}\Psi(x,t)\right)dx}.

Now, we let
C_{3}
be large enough so that the coefficient of the higher order term in (
3.48
) becomes negative. Then (
3.48
) implies for a positive constant
C
that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\sigma x}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}Q\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)dx}\leq C\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\sigma x}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}Q\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)dx}+(C_{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}\|e^{\sigma x}\Psi(x,t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}.

According to Lemma
3.8
and the assumption that
\sigma<\sigma_{0}
, we know that
\|e^{\sigma x}\Psi(x,t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}
is integrable. Moreover, as the initial data is zero for
\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}
and we only consider the case
0<t<1
, we can apply Gronwall’s inequality to obtain that

\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\sigma x}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}Q\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)dx}\leq\int_{0}^{t}e^{C(t-s)}(C_{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}\|e^{\sigma x}\Psi(x,s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}ds\leq\int_{0}^{t}O(1)ds=O(1)t.

Then we apply (
3.47
) again to have

\|e^{\sigma x}\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}=\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\sigma x}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)dx}\leq C_{2}\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\sigma x}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}Q\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)dx}\leq O(1)t.

Next, we consider
-\sigma_{0}<\sigma<0
, and we can obtain the similar estimate as above. Thus we obtain the desired zeroth order estimate as below,

\|e^{\sigma|x|}\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\leq O(1)t,\quad 0<t<1.

Since (
3.36
) is a linear system, the higher order estimates can be similarly constructed. As
\Psi
has finite Sobolev norm up to fourth order, we have the estimates as follows,

\|e^{\sigma|x|}\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\|_{H^{4}(\mathbb{R})}\leq O(1)t,\quad 0<t<1.

Finally, we apply Sobolev inequality and above
L^{2}
norm of higher order derivatives to obtain that

\|\partial^{k}_{x}\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq O(1)te^{-\sigma|x|},\quad 0\leq k\leq 3,\quad 0<t<1.

∎

Next, we study the pointwise structure of
\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}
for
t>1
and the region outside the wave cone. More precisely, we will consider the region
1\leq t
and
|x|>2Mt
, where
M
is a sufficient large positive constant related to the sound speed.

Lemma 3.10
.

Under the assumption (
3.42
), there exist positive constants
\sigma
and
M
such that the following estimates hold when
t\geq 1
,

\|e^{\pm\sigma(x-Mt)}\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}(x,t)\|_{H^{4}(\mathbb{R})}\leq O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t},\quad\sum_{k=0}^{3}|\partial_{x}^{k}\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}(x,t)|\leq O(1)e^{-\frac{\sigma}{2}|x|-\sigma_{0}^{*}t},\quad t\geq 1,\quad|x|>2Mt.

Proof.

We will continue using the notations in Lemma
3.9
. We first choose a sufficiently large constant
M
and a positive constant
\sigma
such that
0<\sigma<\sigma_{0}
. Then, we multiply (
3.44
) by
e^{2\sigma(x-Mt)}(\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}
, take the trace norm and integrate with respect to
x
to have

(3.49)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}}tr\left(e^{2\sigma(x-Mt)}\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}Q\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)\right)dx+2\sigma M\int_{\mathbb{R}}tr\left(e^{2\sigma(x-Mt)}\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}Q\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)\right)dx

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sigma e^{2\sigma(x-Mt)}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}(S_{1})\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\sigma(x-Mt)}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}(P_{1})\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}}2\sigma e^{2\sigma(x-Mt)}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}(P_{1})\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)dx

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\sigma(x-Mt)}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}B(\bar{U})\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{x}\right)dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}}2\sigma^{2}e^{2\sigma(x-Mt)}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}(S_{2})\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}}2\sigma e^{2\sigma(x-Mt)}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}(P_{2})\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{x}\right)dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}tr\left(-e^{2\sigma(x-Mt)}(\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}Q\Psi(x,t)\right)dx.

Comparing to (
3.46
), the only difference is that (
3.49
) contains one more term with coefficient
M
. Then we apply (
3.47
) to have the following estimates,

(3.50)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\sigma x}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}Q\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)dx

\displaystyle\leq\left(3\sigma C_{2}^{2}+2C_{2}^{2}\sigma^{2}+\left(1+2\sigma\right)C_{3}C_{2}^{2}-2\sigma M\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\sigma(x-Mt)}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}Q\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)dx

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{C_{2}}{C_{3}}\left(1+2\sigma\right)-\frac{1}{C_{2}}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\sigma(x-Mt)}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})_{x}^{\mathsf{T}}\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&1\end{pmatrix}\mathbb{G}_{x}^{\dagger}\right)dx

\displaystyle+(C_{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\sigma(x-Mt)}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}Q\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)dx}\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\sigma(x-Mt)}tr\left((\Psi(x,t))^{\mathsf{T}}\Psi(x,t)\right)dx}.

Therefore, we can let
C_{2}\ll C_{3}\ll M
so that the coefficients of zeroth order term and first order term are both negative. Then we have

\frac{d}{dt}\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\sigma(x-Mt)}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}Q\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)dx}\leq-\sigma_{0}^{*}\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\sigma(x-Mt)}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}Q\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)dx}+(C_{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}\|e^{\sigma(x-Mt)}\Psi(x,t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}.

According to Lemma
3.8
and the assumption that
\sigma<\sigma_{0}
, we know that
\|e^{\sigma x}\Psi(x,t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}
is integrable and has time exponential decay. Moreover, the initial data is zero for
\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}
, therefore, we apply Gronwall’s inequality to obtain that

\displaystyle\|e^{\sigma(x-Mt)}\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\sigma(x-Mt)}tr\left((\mathbb{G}^{\dagger})^{\mathsf{T}}Q\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\right)dx}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}(t-s)}(C_{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}\|e^{\sigma(x-Ms)}\Psi(x,s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}ds

\displaystyle\leq O(1)\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}(t-s)}\|e^{\sigma(x-Ms)}e^{-\sigma_{0}x-\sigma_{0}^{*}s}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}ds

\displaystyle\leq O(1)\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}(t-s)}e^{-\sigma Ms}e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}s}ds

\displaystyle\leq O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t}.

Similarly, we can also get

\|e^{\sigma(-x-Mt)}\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\leq O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t}.

Then as in Lemma
3.9
, the higher order estimates can be obtained in a similar way for the linear system (
3.44
).
Finally for
|x|>2Mt
, we apply Sobolev inequality to obtain the
L^{\infty}
estimates as below,

\|\partial_{x}^{k}\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}(x,t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq O(1)e^{-\sigma(|x|-Mt)-\sigma_{0}^{*}t}\leq O(1)e^{-\frac{\sigma|x|}{2}-\sigma_{0}^{*}t},\quad t\geq 1,\quad|x|>2Mt,\quad k=1,2,3.

∎

3.3.2.
Long wave and short wave decomposition

In this part, we will construct the estimates of
\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}
in the region
|x|\leq 2Mt
when
t\geq 1
. First, for full Green’s function
\mathbb{G}
, we can decompose it into long wave part
\mathbb{G}_{L}
and short wave part
\mathbb{G}_{S}
as follows,

(3.51)

\mathbb{G}=\mathbb{G}_{L}+\mathbb{G}_{S},\qquad\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{L}(\eta,t):=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\hat{\mathbb{G}}(\eta,t),\quad|\eta|<\delta,\\
&0,\quad|\eta|\geq\delta,\end{aligned}\right.\qquad\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{S}(\eta,t):=\left\{\begin{aligned} &0,\quad|\eta|<\delta,\\
&\hat{\mathbb{G}}(\eta,t),\quad|\eta|\geq\delta,\end{aligned}\right.

where
\delta
is a sufficient small positive constant. Then, we can similarly define the long wave part
\mathbb{G}^{*}_{L}
(
\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{L}
) and short wave part
\mathbb{G}^{*}_{S}
(
\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{S}
) for
\mathbb{G}^{*}
(
\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}
). From (
3.51
), we immediately have

(3.52)

\mathbb{G}_{L}=\mathbb{G}^{*}_{L}+\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{L},\qquad\mathbb{G}_{S}=\mathbb{G}^{*}_{S}+\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{S}.

In the following, we will study the long wave part and short wave part respectively.

\bullet
(Case 1: Long wave part
\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{L}
) We start from the long wave part of singular part
\mathbb{G}^{*}_{L}
. Actually, we will provide the estimates for
\mathbb{G}
and
\mathbb{G}^{*}
, and then the desired results follows from (
3.52
).

Lemma 3.11
.

The long wave of the singular part
\mathbb{G}^{*}
has the following estimates inside the wave cone when
t\geq 1
,

\left|\partial^{l}_{t}\partial^{k}_{x}\mathbb{G}_{L}^{*}(x,t;\bar{U})\right|\leq O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t}\leq O(1)e^{-\frac{\sigma_{0}^{*}}{2}\left(t+\frac{|x|}{2M}\right)},\quad t\geq 1,\quad|x|\leq 2Mt,\quad l,k\in\mathbb{N}.

Proof.

the Fourier transform of
\mathbb{G}^{*}_{L}
satisfies the formula in (
3.22
), i.e.,

\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{L}^{*}(\eta,t;\bar{U})=\sum_{j=1}^{3}\hat{G}^{*,j}(\eta,t;\bar{U})=\sum_{j=1}^{3}e^{\lambda_{j}^{*}t}\hat{\mathbb{M}}_{j}^{*},\quad|\eta|<\delta.

According to Lemma
3.3
,
\lambda^{*}_{j}
has spectral gap such that
Re(\lambda^{*}_{j})<-\sigma_{0}^{*}
for
j=1,2,3
. Moreover,
\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{*}_{L}(\eta,t)
vanishes when
|\eta|\geq\delta
, and
\hat{\mathbb{M}}_{j}^{*}=O(1)
when
|\eta|<\delta
. Therefore, we take inverse Fourier transform of
\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{L}^{*}(\eta,t;\bar{U})
to obtain

|\mathbb{G}_{L}^{*}(x,t;\bar{U})|\leq O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t}\leq O(1)e^{-\frac{\sigma_{0}^{*}}{2}\left(t+\frac{|x|}{2M}\right)},\quad t\geq 1,\quad|x|\leq 2Mt.

where the last inequality comes from the fact
|x|\leq 2Mt
. For higher order estimates
\partial^{l}_{t}\partial^{k}_{x}\mathbb{G}_{L}^{*}(x,t;\bar{U})
, we need to multiply
(i\eta)^{k}
or
(\lambda_{j}^{*})^{l}
to
\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{*}
and take inverse Fourier transform. Since
|\eta|\leq\delta
for long wave, the desired higher order estimates follow similar arguments in zeroth order estimates, and we omit the details.
∎

Next, we need the estimates of the long wave part of the full Green’s function
\mathbb{G}
. Actually, we have the asymptotic expansion of the eigenvalues
\lambda_{j}
near zero in (
3.15
), (
3.16
) and (
3.17
). Moreover, if we expand up to second order, then we have a simpler formula as follows,

(3.53)

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}=i\eta\beta_{1}-\alpha_{1}\eta^{2}+O(1)\eta^{3},\quad\lambda_{2}=i\eta\beta_{2}-\alpha_{2}\eta^{2}+O(1)\eta^{3},\quad\lambda_{3}=i\eta\beta_{3}-\alpha_{3}\eta^{2}+O(1)\eta^{3}.

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}=\frac{-\kappa\theta_{e}p_{v}}{v\left(pp_{e}-p_{v}\right)},\quad\alpha_{2}=\alpha_{3}=\frac{\left(\kappa p\theta_{e}p_{e}+\mu pp_{e}-\mu p_{v}\right)}{2v\left(pp_{e}-p_{v}\right)},\quad\beta_{1}=0,\quad\beta_{2}=-\sqrt{pp_{e}-p_{v}},\quad\beta_{3}=\sqrt{pp_{e}-p_{v}},

where
\alpha_{j}>0
and
\beta_{j}
are real numbers as we discussed before. Then, similar as in Lemma
3.4
, the expansion of
\lambda_{j}
yields the asymptotical expansion of
\hat{M}_{j}
at zero. In fact, we have

(3.54)

\hat{\mathbb{M}}_{j}=M_{j}^{0}+i\eta M_{j}^{1}+O(1)\eta^{2},\quad j=1,2,3,

where
M_{j}^{k}
are listed in Appendix
C
. Now, we combine the explicit expression of full Green’s function in (
3.9
), the expansion of eigenvalue in (
3.53
) and the expansion of
\hat{\mathbb{M}}_{j}
in (
3.54
) to have for
|\eta|<\delta
that,

(3.55)

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{L}(\eta,t)

\displaystyle=\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{L}^{1}+\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{L}^{2}+\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{L}^{3}

\displaystyle=e^{-\alpha_{1}\eta^{2}t+O(1)\eta^{3}t}\left(M_{1}^{0}+i\eta M_{1}^{1}+O(1)\eta^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+e^{i\eta\beta_{2}t-\alpha_{2}\eta^{2}t+O(1)\eta^{3}t}\left(M_{2}^{0}+i\eta M_{2}^{1}+O(1)\eta^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+e^{i\eta\beta_{3}t-\alpha_{3}\eta^{2}t+O(1)\eta^{3}t}\left(M_{3}^{0}+i\eta M_{3}^{1}+O(1)\eta^{2}\right).

Lemma 3.12
.

Suppose
t\geq 1
and
|x|\leq 2Mt
. Then there exists a positive constant
C
such that, the long wave part of the full Green’s function has the following estimate for
j=1,2,3
and
k\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{x}^{k}\mathbb{G}_{L}^{j}(x,t)-\partial_{x}^{k}\left(\frac{e^{-\frac{(x+\beta_{j}t)^{2}}{4\alpha_{j}t}}}{2\sqrt{\pi\alpha_{j}t}}\right)M_{j}^{0}-\partial^{k+1}_{x}\left(\frac{e^{-\frac{(x+\beta_{j}t)^{2}}{4\alpha_{j}t}}}{2\sqrt{\pi\alpha_{j}t}}\right)M_{j}^{1}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt\leq\frac{O(1)e^{-\frac{(x+\beta_{j}t)^{2}}{4Ct}}}{t^{\frac{k+2}{2}}}M_{j}^{0}+\frac{O(1)e^{-\frac{(x+\beta_{j}t)^{2}}{4Ct}}}{t^{\frac{k+3}{2}}}+O(1)e^{-\alpha_{j}\delta^{2}(\frac{t}{2}+\frac{|x|}{4M})}.

Proof.

According to the discussion at the beginning of Section
3
, we already knew
\lambda_{j}
are analytic functions of
\eta
when
|\eta|\ll 1
. Therefore, in (
3.55
), we can expand the exponential part of
\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{L}^{1}
and apply the mean value theorem to find
\bar{\eta}
between
0
and
\eta
such that

(3.56)

e^{\lambda_{1}t}=e^{-\alpha_{1}\eta^{2}t}\left(1+e^{O(1)\bar{\eta}^{3}t}O(1)\eta^{3}t\right)=e^{-\alpha_{1}\eta^{2}t}+O(1)\eta e^{-\bar{\alpha}_{1}\eta^{2}t},

where
\bar{\alpha}_{1}
is some positive constant such that
0<\bar{\alpha}_{1}<\alpha_{1}
. The last equality holds because
|\bar{\eta}|\leq|\eta|<\delta
, and thus we can choose a sufficiently small
\bar{\alpha}_{1}
and use the term
e^{-(\alpha_{1}-\bar{\alpha}_{1})\eta^{2}t}
to absorb the part
\eta^{2}te^{O(1)\bar{\eta}^{3}t}
. Without loss of generality, we may let
\delta
be sufficiently small and choose
\bar{\alpha}_{1}=\frac{\alpha_{1}}{2}
. Now we combine (
3.55
) and (
3.56
) to obtain that

(3.57)

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{L}^{1}(\eta,t)

\displaystyle=\left(e^{-\alpha_{1}\eta^{2}t}+O(1)\eta e^{-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{2}\eta^{2}t}\right)\left(M_{1}^{0}+i\eta M_{1}^{1}+O(1)\eta^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(e^{-\alpha_{1}\eta^{2}t}+O(1)\eta e^{-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{2}\eta^{2}t}\right)M_{1}^{0}+i\eta e^{-\alpha_{1}\eta^{2}t}M_{1}^{1}+O(1)\eta^{2}\left(e^{-\alpha_{1}\eta^{2}t}+e^{-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{2}\eta^{2}t}\right),\quad|\eta|<\delta.

We will take inverse Fourier transform of above formula term by term.

\diamond
(Step 1: Leading term
e^{-\alpha_{1}\eta^{2}t}
) : First, when
|\eta|<\delta
, the leading term
e^{-\alpha_{1}\eta^{2}t}
is exactly the heat kernel. While when
|\eta|>\delta
, the long wave part vanishes due to (
3.51
). Therefore, the leading term of
\hat{\mathbb{G}}_{L}^{1}(\eta,t)
has inverse Fourier transform as follows,

(3.58)

\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}e^{i\eta x-\alpha_{1}\eta^{2}t}d\eta=\frac{1}{2\pi}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha_{1}t}}\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}e^{-\alpha_{1}t\left(\eta-i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}\right)^{2}}d\eta.

Now, as the term
e^{-\alpha_{1}t\left(\eta-i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}\right)^{2}}
is analytic as a function of
\eta
, we can use contour integral and the change of variable to rewrite the above integration (
3.58
) as follows,

(3.59)

\displaystyle\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}e^{-\alpha_{1}t\left(\eta-i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}\right)^{2}}d\eta

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{\delta}^{\delta+i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}+\int_{\delta+i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}^{-\delta+i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}+\int_{-\delta+i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}^{-\delta}\right)e^{-\alpha_{1}t\left(\eta-i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}\right)^{2}}d\eta

\displaystyle=\left(-\int_{\delta}^{\delta+i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}+\int^{+\infty+i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}_{-\infty+i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}-\int_{\delta+i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}^{+\infty+i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}-\int^{-\delta+i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}_{-\infty+i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}+\int^{-\delta+i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}_{-\delta}\right)e^{-\alpha_{1}t\left(\eta-i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}\right)^{2}}d\eta

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}e^{-\alpha_{1}t\nu^{2}}d\nu-\int_{|\nu|>\delta}e^{-\alpha_{1}t\nu^{2}}d\nu+i\int^{\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}_{0}e^{-\alpha_{1}t\left(\delta+i\nu\right)^{2}}d\nu-i\int^{\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}_{0}e^{-\alpha_{1}t\left(\delta-i\nu\right)^{2}}d\nu.

We can directly calculate the first term, and for the second term, we have a spectral gap with order
\delta
which provides a exponential decay. More precisely, we have

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}e^{-\alpha_{1}t\nu^{2}}d\nu=\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{\alpha_{1}t}},

\displaystyle\int_{|\nu|>\delta}e^{-\alpha_{1}t\nu^{2}}d\nu=e^{-\alpha_{1}\delta^{2}t}\int_{|\nu|>\delta}e^{-\alpha_{1}t(|\nu|-\delta)(|\nu|+\delta)}d\nu\leq e^{-\alpha_{1}\delta^{2}t}\int_{|\nu|>\delta}e^{-\alpha_{1}\delta t(|\nu|-\delta)}d\nu=\frac{2e^{-\alpha_{1}\delta^{2}t}}{\alpha_{1}\delta t}.

For the third and fourth terms in (
3.59
), it is obviously they are conjugate and thus we can combine them together to obtain the following estimate,

\displaystyle\left|i\int^{\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}_{0}e^{-\alpha_{1}t\left(\delta+i\nu\right)^{2}}d\nu-i\int^{\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}_{0}e^{-\alpha_{1}t\left(\delta-i\nu\right)^{2}}d\nu\right|

\displaystyle=\left|i\int^{\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}_{0}e^{-\alpha_{1}\delta^{2}t+\alpha_{1}\nu^{2}t}\left(e^{-2i\alpha_{1}\delta\nu t}-e^{2i\alpha_{1}\delta\nu t}\right)d\nu\right|\leq 2\left|\int^{\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}_{0}e^{-\alpha_{1}\delta^{2}t+\alpha_{1}\nu^{2}t}d\nu\right|\leq\frac{|x|}{\alpha_{1}t}e^{-\alpha_{1}\delta^{2}t}e^{\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha_{1}t}}.

Combining (
3.58
), (
3.59
) and above estimates of (
3.59
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}e^{i\eta x-\alpha_{1}\eta^{2}t}d\eta-\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi\alpha_{1}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha_{1}t}}\right|\leq\frac{1}{2\pi}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha_{1}t}}\left(\frac{2e^{-\alpha_{1}\delta^{2}t}}{\alpha_{1}\delta t}\right)+\frac{|x|}{2\pi\alpha_{1}t}e^{-\alpha_{1}\delta^{2}t}.

As we consider the region
t\geq 1
and
|x|\leq 2Mt
, we can further use the exponential decay
e^{-\alpha_{1}\delta^{2}t}
to absorb the
|x|
and
\frac{1}{t}
. Then we obtain

(3.60)

\left|\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}e^{i\eta x-\alpha_{1}\eta^{2}t}d\eta-\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi\alpha_{1}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha_{1}t}}\right|\leq O(1)e^{-\alpha_{1}\delta^{2}(\frac{t}{2}+\frac{|x|}{4M})}.

\diamond
(Step 2: Higher order terms) The higher order terms are similar as derivatives of the heat kernel. Similarly as before, as the long wave only contains low frequency part, we have to estimate the remainders. Actually, we have

(3.61)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}i\eta e^{i\eta x-\alpha_{1}\eta^{2}t}d\eta

\displaystyle=-\frac{x}{4\pi\alpha_{1}t}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha_{1}t}}\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}e^{-\alpha_{1}t\left(\eta-i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}\right)^{2}}d\eta+\frac{1}{2\pi}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha_{1}t}}\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}i\left(\eta-i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}\right)e^{-\alpha_{1}t\left(\eta-i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}\right)^{2}}d\eta.

The estimate of the first term in (
3.61
) directly follows from the estimates of the leading term in Step 1. For the second term, as it is analytic in
\eta
, we can apply contour integral similarly as before to obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}i\left(\eta-i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}\right)e^{-\alpha_{1}t\left(\eta-i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}\right)^{2}}d\eta

\displaystyle=\left(-\int_{\delta}^{\delta+i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}+\int^{+\infty+i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}_{-\infty+i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}-\int_{\delta+i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}^{+\infty+i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}-\int^{-\delta+i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}_{-\infty+i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}+\int^{-\delta+i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}_{-\delta}\right)i\left(\eta-i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}\right)e^{-\alpha_{1}t\left(\eta-i\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}\right)^{2}}d\eta

\displaystyle=i\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\nu e^{-\alpha_{1}t\nu^{2}}d\nu-i\int_{|\nu|>\delta}\nu e^{-\alpha_{1}t\nu^{2}}d\nu+\int^{\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}_{0}\left(i\nu+\delta\right)e^{-\alpha_{1}t\left(\delta+i\nu\right)^{2}}d\nu-\int^{\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}_{0}\left(i\nu-\delta\right)e^{-\alpha_{1}t\left(\delta-i\nu\right)^{2}}d\nu.

Then, the first two terms are zero due the symmetric property, the last two terms can be treated similar as before. In fact, we have

\displaystyle\left|\int^{\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}_{0}\left(i\nu+\delta\right)e^{-\alpha_{1}t\left(\delta+i\nu\right)^{2}}d\nu-\int^{\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}_{0}\left(i\nu-\delta\right)e^{-\alpha_{1}t\left(\delta-i\nu\right)^{2}}d\nu\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\int^{\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}_{0}i\nu e^{-\alpha_{1}\delta^{2}t+\alpha_{1}\nu^{2}t}\left(e^{-2i\alpha_{1}\delta\nu t}-e^{2i\alpha_{1}\delta\nu t}\right)d\nu\right|+\left|\int^{\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}_{0}\delta e^{-\alpha_{1}\delta^{2}t+\alpha_{1}\nu^{2}t}\left(e^{-2i\alpha_{1}\delta\nu t}+e^{2i\alpha_{1}\delta\nu t}\right)d\nu\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left|\int^{\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}_{0}i\nu e^{-\alpha_{1}\delta^{2}t+\alpha_{1}\nu^{2}t}d\nu\right|+2\left|\int^{\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}}_{0}\delta e^{-\alpha_{1}\delta^{2}t+\alpha_{1}\nu^{2}t}d\nu\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|x|^{2}}{(2\alpha_{1}t)^{2}}e^{-\alpha_{1}\delta^{2}t}e^{\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha_{1}t}}+\frac{\delta|x|}{\alpha_{1}t}e^{-\alpha_{1}\delta^{2}t}e^{\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha_{1}t}}.

Combing (
3.60
), (
3.61
) and above estimates, and similarly as in the end of Step 1, we apply the property
t\geq 1
and
|x|\leq 2Mt
to have

(3.62)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}i\eta e^{i\eta x-\alpha_{1}\eta^{2}t}d\eta-\partial_{x}\left(\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi\alpha_{1}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha_{1}t}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}i\eta e^{i\eta x-\alpha_{1}\eta^{2}t}d\eta+\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}\left(\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi\alpha_{1}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha_{1}t}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{x}{2\alpha_{1}t}\left(O(1)e^{-\alpha_{1}\delta^{2}(\frac{t}{2}+\frac{|x|}{4M})}\right)+\frac{1}{2\pi}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha_{1}t}}\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{(2\alpha_{1}t)^{2}}e^{-\alpha_{1}\delta^{2}t}e^{\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha_{1}t}}+\frac{\delta|x|}{\alpha_{1}t}e^{-\alpha_{1}\delta^{2}t}e^{\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha_{1}t}}\right)

\displaystyle=O(1)e^{-\alpha_{1}\delta^{2}(\frac{t}{2}+\frac{|x|}{4M})}.

Moreover, if only concerning about the decay estimate, we can rewrite (
3.62
) as follows,

\left|\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}i\eta e^{i\eta x-\alpha_{1}\eta^{2}t}d\eta\right|\leq\frac{|x|}{2\alpha_{1}t}\left(\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi\alpha_{1}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha_{1}t}}\right)+O(1)e^{-\alpha_{1}\delta^{2}(\frac{t}{2}+\frac{|x|}{4M})}.

Then, as
\frac{|x|}{\sqrt{t}}
can be absorbed by a small part from
e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha_{1}t}}
to bounded, we can find a proper positive constant
C>\alpha_{1}
such that

(3.63)

\left|\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}i\eta e^{i\eta x-\alpha_{1}\eta^{2}t}d\eta\right|\leq\frac{O(1)}{t}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4Ct}}+O(1)e^{-\alpha_{1}\delta^{2}(\frac{t}{2}+\frac{|x|}{4M})}.

Then, the higher order estimates are followed by similar manner. We omit the details and obtain that

(3.64)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\left|\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}(i\eta)^{k}e^{i\eta x-\alpha_{1}\eta^{2}t}d\eta-\partial^{k}_{x}\left(\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi\alpha_{1}t}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4\alpha_{1}t}}\right)\right|\leq O(1)e^{-\alpha_{1}\delta^{2}(\frac{t}{2}+\frac{|x|}{4M})},\\
&\left|\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}(i\eta)^{k}e^{i\eta x-\alpha_{1}\eta^{2}t}d\eta|\right|\leq\frac{O(1)}{t^{\frac{k+1}{2}}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4Ct}}+O(1)e^{-\alpha_{1}\delta^{2}(\frac{t}{2}+\frac{|x|}{4M})}.\end{aligned}\right.

Finally, we combine (
3.57
) and (
3.64
) to obtain the desired estimates.
∎

Now we combine the estimates for
\mathbb{G}^{*}_{L}
in Lemma
3.11
and the estimates of
\mathbb{G}_{L}
in Lemma
3.12
to construct the estimates of
\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{L}
.

Lemma 3.13
.

When
t\geq 1
and
|x|\leq 2Mt
, there exist sufficiently small positive constant
\sigma_{0}^{*}
and large constant
C
such that, the long wave of the regular part
\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{L}(x,t)
has the following estimates

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{x}^{k}\mathbb{G}_{L}^{\dagger}(x,t)-\sum_{j=1}^{3}\partial_{x}^{k}\left(\frac{e^{-\frac{(x+\beta_{j}t)^{2}}{4\alpha_{j}t}}}{2\sqrt{\pi\alpha_{j}t}}\right)M_{j}^{0}-\sum_{j=1}^{3}\partial^{k+1}_{x}\left(\frac{e^{-\frac{(x+\beta_{j}t)^{2}}{4\alpha_{j}t}}}{2\sqrt{\pi\alpha_{j}t}}\right)M_{j}^{1}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt\leq\sum_{j=1}^{3}\frac{O(1)e^{-\frac{(x+\beta_{j}t)^{2}}{4Ct}}}{t^{\frac{k+2}{2}}}M_{j}^{0}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\frac{O(1)e^{-\frac{(x+\beta_{j}t)^{2}}{4Ct}}}{t^{\frac{k+3}{2}}}+O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}(\frac{t}{2}+\frac{|x|}{4M})}.

\bullet
(Case 2: Short wave part
\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{S}
)
In this part, we will study the short wave part
\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{S}
. From the equation (
3.36
), we have the following equation of
\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\dagger}
,

\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\dagger}_{t}+i\eta F^{\prime}(\bar{U})\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\dagger}+\eta^{2}B(\bar{U})\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\dagger}=-\hat{\Psi}(\eta,t),

with zero initial data. For the homogeneous equation, we already have the solution operator in (
3.9
), therefore we apply Duhamel’s principle to obtain the solution to the inhomogeneous equation, and express
\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\dagger}_{S}
as follows,

\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\dagger}_{S}(\eta,t)=-\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{j=1}^{3}e^{\lambda_{j}(t-\tau)}\hat{\mathbb{M}}_{j}\hat{\Psi}(\eta,\tau)d\tau,\quad|\eta|\geq\delta.

Lemma 3.14
.

There exists a sufficiently small positive constant
\sigma_{0}^{*}
such that, when
t\geq 1
and
|x|\leq 2Mt
, the short wave part
\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{S}
has the following estimates,

\displaystyle|\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\dagger}_{S}(\eta,t)|=O(1)\frac{e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t}}{(1+|\eta|)^{6}},

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{S}(x,t)\|_{H^{4}(\mathbb{R})}\leq O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t},\quad\sum_{k=0}^{3}\left|\partial^{k}_{x}\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{S}(x,t)\right|\leq O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t}\leq O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}\left(\frac{t}{2}+\frac{|x|}{4M}\right)},\quad t\geq 1,\quad|x|\leq 2Mt.

Proof.

From Lemma
3.8
, we know the source term
\hat{\Psi}
exponentially decays with respect to
t
, and has a small tail for large
\eta
. On the other hand, according to Lemma
3.2
, there is a spectral gap for
r<|\eta|<R
, where
r
and
R
are two arbitrary positive constants. Moreover, according to Lemma
3.3
, there is also a spectral gap for sufficiently large
\eta
. Therefore, without loss of generality, we may let
\sigma_{0}^{*}
be sufficiently small so that
Re(\lambda_{j})<-\sigma_{0}^{*}
for
|\eta|\geq\delta
. Finally,
\hat{\mathbb{M}}_{j}
is bounded when
|\eta|
near infinity from the expansion, and it is also bounded for
\eta
in any finite region because
\lambda_{j}(\eta)
is continuous as we discussed in Section
3
. Therefore,
\hat{\mathbb{M}}_{j}
is simply an order one term. Then, we combine the spectral gap,
O(1)
estimates of
\hat{\mathbb{M}}_{j}
and Lemma
3.8
to have

\displaystyle|\hat{\mathbb{G}}^{\dagger}_{S}(\eta,t)|=|\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{j=1}^{3}e^{\lambda_{j}(t-\tau)}\hat{\mathbb{M}}_{j}\hat{\Psi}(\eta,\tau)d\tau|\leq O(1)\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{j=1}^{3}e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}(t-\tau)}e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}\tau}(1+|\eta|)^{-6}d\tau=O(1)\frac{e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t}}{(1+|\eta|)^{6}}.

As we have the decay estimate of
\eta
up to order six when
|\eta|
tends to infinity, the Plancherel’s theorem immediately yields that

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{S}(x,t)\|_{H^{4}(\mathbb{R})}\leq O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t},\quad\sum_{k=0}^{3}\left|\partial^{k}_{x}\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}_{S}(x,t)\right|\leq O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t}\leq O(1)e^{-\left(\frac{\sigma_{0}^{*}t}{2}+\frac{|x|}{4M}\right)},\quad t\geq 1,\quad|x|\leq 2Mt.

∎

3.3.3.
Pointwise estimate of
\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}(x,t,\bar{U})

In the final part of this subsection, we will combine Lemma
3.9
(
t<1
), Lemma
3.10
(
t\geq 1
and
|x|>2Mt
), Lemma
3.13
(
t\geq 1
,
|x|\leq 2Mt
and
|\eta|<\delta
) and Lemma
3.14
(
t\geq 1
,
|x|\leq 2Mt
and
|\eta|\geq\delta
) to construct the full estimates of the regular part
\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}(x,t)
.

Theorem 3.3
.

Suppose the assumption (
3.42
) holds. Then we can find a sufficiently large constant
C
, sufficiently small positive constant
\sigma_{0}
and
\sigma^{*}_{0}
such that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{3}|\partial_{x}^{k}\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}(x,t)|\leq O(1)te^{-\sigma_{0}|x|},

\displaystyle\quad 0<t<1,

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{x}^{k}\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}(x,t)-\sum_{j=1}^{3}\partial_{x}^{k}\left(\frac{e^{-\frac{(x+\beta_{j}t)^{2}}{4\alpha_{j}t}}}{2\sqrt{\pi\alpha_{j}t}}\right)M_{j}^{0}-\sum_{j=1}^{3}\partial^{k+1}_{x}\left(\frac{e^{-\frac{(x+\beta_{j}t)^{2}}{4\alpha_{j}t}}}{2\sqrt{\pi\alpha_{j}t}}\right)M_{j}^{1}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\leq\sum_{j=1}^{3}\frac{O(1)e^{-\frac{(x+\beta_{j}t)^{2}}{4Ct}}}{t^{\frac{k+2}{2}}}M_{j}^{0}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\frac{O(1)e^{-\frac{(x+\beta_{j}t)^{2}}{4Ct}}}{t^{\frac{k+3}{2}}}+O(1)e^{-\sigma_{0}^{*}t-\sigma_{0}|x|},

\displaystyle\quad t\geq 1,\quad 0\leq k\leq 3,

where
\alpha_{j}
and
\beta_{j}
are given below,

\alpha_{1}=\frac{-\kappa\theta_{e}p_{v}}{v\left(pp_{e}-p_{v}\right)},\quad\alpha_{2}=\alpha_{3}=\frac{\left(\kappa p\theta_{e}p_{e}+\mu pp_{e}-\mu p_{v}\right)}{2v\left(pp_{e}-p_{v}\right)},\quad\beta_{1}=0,\quad\beta_{2}=-\sqrt{pp_{e}-p_{v}},\quad\beta_{3}=\sqrt{pp_{e}-p_{v}}.

Proof.

The proof directly follows from Lemma
3.9
, Lemma
3.10
, Lemma
3.13
and Lemma
3.14
. We emphasize that,
\sigma_{0}
and
\sigma_{0}^{*}
are first introduced in Lemma
3.3
, but we can choose them further smaller so that all the previous estimates hold.
∎

4.
Global well-posedness

In this section, we will show the local-in-time solution constructed in
[
21
]
(see Propositions
1.1
and
1.2
) can be extended to exist globally in time. To this end, we will take use of the Green’s function constructed in Section
3
. For simplicity, we assume the initial data to be a small perturbation of the constant state
(\bar{v},\bar{u},\bar{\theta})=(1,0,1)
. Then, if we use total energy
E
as a variable, we actually have

\bar{U}=(\bar{v},\bar{u},\bar{E})=(1,0,c_{v}),

where
c_{v}
is the heat capacity defined in (
1.2
), and
\bar{U}
is the equilibrium state in (
1.14
). In this setting, the expression of the Green’s function
\mathbb{G}
would be much simplified. Actually, recall the singular part
\mathbb{G}^{*}
and the regular part
\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}
in Section
3
, the matrix
\mathbb{G}
can be represented as follows,

(4.1)

\mathbb{G}=\mathbb{G}^{*}+\mathbb{G}^{\dagger},\quad\mathbb{G}^{*}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathbb{G}_{11}^{*}&\mathbb{G}_{12}^{*}&\mathbb{G}_{13}^{*}\\
\mathbb{G}_{21}^{*}&\mathbb{G}_{22}^{*}&\mathbb{G}_{23}^{*}\\
\mathbb{G}_{31}^{*}&\mathbb{G}_{32}^{*}&\mathbb{G}_{33}^{*}\end{array}\right),\quad\mathbb{G}^{\dagger}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathbb{G}_{11}^{\dagger}&\mathbb{G}_{12}^{\dagger}&\mathbb{G}_{13}^{\dagger}\\
\mathbb{G}_{21}^{\dagger}&\mathbb{G}_{22}^{\dagger}&\mathbb{G}_{23}^{\dagger}\\
\mathbb{G}_{31}^{\dagger}&\mathbb{G}_{32}^{\dagger}&\mathbb{G}_{33}^{\dagger}\end{array}\right).

Since
\mathbb{G}(x,t;\bar{U})
satisfies the forward equation (
3.1
), it follows that
\mathbb{G}(x-y,t-\tau;\bar{U})
also satisfies the following backward equation,

(4.2)

\partial_{\tau}\mathbb{G}(x-y,t-\tau;\bar{U})+\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}(x-y,t-\tau;\bar{U})F^{\prime}(\bar{U})+\partial_{y}^{2}\mathbb{G}(x-y,t-\tau;\bar{U})B(\bar{U})=0.

In the following, we will briefly denote the Green’s function by
\mathbb{G}(x-y,t-\tau)
. As we already chose
\bar{U}=(\bar{v},\bar{u},\bar{E})=(1,0,c_{v})
, the matrices
F^{\prime}(\bar{U})
and
B(\bar{U})
have the following form,

F^{\prime}(\bar{U})=\begin{pmatrix}0&-1&0\\
-K&0&\frac{K}{c_{v}}\\
0&K&0\end{pmatrix},\quad B(\bar{U})=\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&\mu&0\\
0&0&\frac{\kappa}{c_{v}}\end{pmatrix}.

Now, we substitute above
F^{\prime}(\bar{U})
and
B(\bar{U})
into the backward equation (
4.2
) to obtain the following equations for each component,

(4.3)

\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\partial_{\tau}\mathbb{G}_{11}-K\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{12}&\partial_{\tau}\mathbb{G}_{12}-\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{11}+K\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{13}+\mu\partial_{y}^{2}\mathbb{G}_{12}&\partial_{\tau}\mathbb{G}_{13}+\frac{K}{c_{v}}\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{12}+\frac{\kappa}{c_{v}}\partial_{y}^{2}\mathbb{G}_{13}\\
&&\\
\partial_{\tau}\mathbb{G}_{21}-K\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{22}&\partial_{\tau}\mathbb{G}_{22}-\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{21}+K\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{23}+\mu\partial_{y}^{2}\mathbb{G}_{22}&\partial_{\tau}\mathbb{G}_{23}+\frac{K}{c_{v}}\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{22}+\frac{\kappa}{c_{v}}\partial_{y}^{2}\mathbb{G}_{23}\\
&&\\
\partial_{\tau}\mathbb{G}_{31}-K\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{32}&\partial_{\tau}\mathbb{G}_{32}-\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{31}+K\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{33}+\mu\partial_{y}^{2}\mathbb{G}_{32}&\partial_{\tau}\mathbb{G}_{33}+\frac{K}{c_{v}}\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{32}+\frac{\kappa}{c_{v}}\partial_{y}^{2}\mathbb{G}_{33}\\
\end{array}\right)=0.

4.1.
Representation by Green’s function

The representation of the solution in terms of variable coefficient heat kernel in Section
2
is mainly used to construct the local solution, but not convenient to get large time structure. In this part, we also include the Green’s function into the representations. Now, in order to construct the new representation of
v
,
u
and
\theta
by Green’s function, we first introduce an effective Green’s function
G
as in
[
15
]
. We define a smooth non-increasing cutoff function as follows,

(4.4)

\mathcal{X}(t)\in C^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}_{+}\right),\quad\mathcal{X}^{\prime}(t)\leq 0,\quad\|\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\leq 2,\quad\mathcal{X}(t)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &1,\quad\text{for $t\in(0,1]$,}\\
&0,\quad\text{for $t>2$.}\end{aligned}\right.

Then, we choose a small positive constant
\nu_{0}
such that, the heat kernel
H(x,t;y,\tau;\frac{1}{v})
and the local weak solution
(v(x,\tau),u(x,\tau),E(x,\tau))
for (
1.1
) both exist when
\tau\in(t-2\nu_{0},t]
. We interpolate the heat kernel for short time and Green’s function for large time via cutoff function (
4.4
), thus introduce the effective Green’s functions as follows,

(4.5)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &G_{22}(x,t;y,\tau)=\mathcal{X}\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\nu_{0}}\right)H\left(x,t;y,\tau;\frac{\mu}{v}\right)+\left(1-\mathcal{X}\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\nu_{0}}\right)\right)\mathbb{G}_{22}(x-y;t-\tau),\\
&G_{33}(x,t;y,\tau)=\mathcal{X}\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\nu_{0}}\right)H\left(x,t;y,\tau;\frac{\kappa}{c_{v}v}\right)+\left(1-\mathcal{X}\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\nu_{0}}\right)\right)\mathbb{G}_{33}(x-y;t-\tau).\end{aligned}\right.

Now we can represent the solution
(v,u,E)
in terms of the effective Green’s function, which captures both the local-in-time regularity and global-in-time space-time structure of the solution.

Lemma 4.1
.

Suppose the weak solution
(v(x,\tau),u(x,\tau),E(x,\tau))
for (
1.1
) exists for
\tau\in[0,t]
, and the heat kernel
H(x,t;y,\tau;\frac{\mu}{v})
exists for
\tau\in(t-2\nu_{0},t)
for a sufficiently small positive constant
\nu_{0}
such that
2\nu_{0}<t
. Then we have the representation of
u(x,t)
as follows,

(4.6)

\displaystyle u(x,t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{G}_{21}(x-y,t)(v(y,0)-1)dy+\int_{\mathbb{R}}G_{22}(x,t;y,0)u(y,0)dy+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{G}_{23}(x-y,t)\left(E(y,0)-c_{v}\right)dy+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\mathcal{R}^{u}_{i},

where the inhomogeneous remainders
\mathcal{R}_{i}
are listed as below,

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{u}_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t-2\nu_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{22}(x-y,t-\tau)\left(\frac{K(v-1)^{2}}{v}+\frac{K(\theta-1)(1-v)}{v}-\frac{Ku^{2}}{2c_{v}}+\frac{\mu u_{y}(v-1)}{v}\right)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{0}^{t-2\nu_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{23}(x-y,t-\tau)\left(\left(\frac{K(\theta-1)+K(1-v)}{v}\right)u+\frac{\kappa\theta_{y}(v-1)}{v}+\left(\frac{\kappa}{c_{v}}-\frac{\mu}{v}\right)uu_{y}\right)dyd\tau,

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{u}_{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{t-2\nu_{0}}^{t-\nu_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{22}(x-y,t-\tau)\left(\frac{K(v-1)^{2}}{v}+\frac{K(\theta-1)(1-v)}{v}+\frac{\mu u_{y}(v-1)}{v}\left(1-\mathcal{X}\right)-\frac{Ku^{2}}{2c_{v}}\right)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{t-2\nu_{0}}^{t-\nu_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{\nu_{0}}\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\nu_{0}}\right)\left(\mathbb{G}_{22}(x-y;t-\tau)-H\left(x,t;y,\tau;\frac{\mu}{v}\right)\right)u(y,\tau)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{t-2\nu_{0}}^{t-\nu_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{X}\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\nu_{0}}\right)\left(K\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{23}(x-y,t-\tau)-\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{21}(x-y,t-\tau)\right)u(y,\tau)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{t-2\nu_{0}}^{t-\nu_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{X}\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\nu_{0}}\right)\left(H_{y}\left(x,t;y,\tau;\frac{\mu}{v}\right)-\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{22}(x-y;t-\tau)\right)\frac{K(\theta-v)}{v}dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{t-2\nu_{0}}^{t-\nu_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{23}(x-y;t-\tau)\left(\frac{\kappa\theta_{y}(v-1)}{v}+\frac{Ku(\theta-v)}{v}+\left(\frac{\kappa}{c_{v}}-\frac{\mu}{v}\right)uu_{y}\right)dyd\tau,

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{u}_{3}

\displaystyle=\int_{t-\nu_{0}}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}K\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{22}(x-y,t-\tau)(v(y,\tau)-1)dyd\tau-\int_{t-\nu_{0}}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{21}(x-y,t-\tau)u(y,\tau)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{t-\nu_{0}}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}H_{y}\left(x,t;y,\tau;\frac{\mu}{v}\right)\left(\frac{K\theta(y,\tau)-Kv(y,\tau)}{v(y,\tau)}\right)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt-\int_{t-\nu_{0}}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{22}(x-y,t-\tau)\left(K(\theta-1)+\frac{Ku^{2}}{2c_{v}}\right)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{t-\nu_{0}}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{23}(x-y,t-\tau)\left((pu)+\frac{\kappa(v-1)\theta_{y}}{v}+\left(\frac{\kappa}{c_{v}}-\frac{\mu}{v}\right)uu_{y}\right)dyd\tau.

Proof.

In fact, we multiply the vector
\left(\mathbb{G}_{21}(x-y,t-\tau),G_{22}(x,t;y,\tau),\mathbb{G}_{23}(x-y,t-\tau)\right)
to the system (
1.1
), and apply the integration by part to have

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{G}_{21}(x-y,t-\tau)\left(v_{\tau}(y,\tau)-u_{y}(y,\tau)\right)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}G_{22}(x,t;y,\tau)\left(u_{\tau}+p_{y}-\left(\frac{\mu u_{y}}{v}\right)_{y}\right)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{G}_{23}(x-y,t-\tau)\left(E_{\tau}+(pu)_{y}-\left(\frac{\kappa}{v}\theta_{y}+\frac{\mu}{v}uu_{y}\right)_{y}\right)dyd\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{G}_{21}(x-y,t-\tau)\left((v-1)_{\tau}(y,\tau)-u_{y}(y,\tau)\right)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}G_{22}(x,t;y,\tau)\left(u_{\tau}+(p(v,\theta)-p(1,1))_{y}-\left(\frac{\mu u_{y}}{v}\right)_{y}\right)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{G}_{23}(x-y,t-\tau)\left((E-c_{v})_{\tau}+(pu)_{y}-\left(\frac{\kappa}{v}\theta_{y}+\frac{\mu}{v}uu_{y}\right)_{y}\right)dyd\tau

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{G}_{21}(x-y,t)(v_{0}(y)-1)dy-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tau}\mathbb{G}_{21}(x-y,t-\tau)(v(y,\tau)-1)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{21}(x-y,t-\tau)u(y,\tau)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+u(x,t)-\int_{\mathbb{R}}G_{22}(x,t;y,0)u(y,0)dy-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tau}G_{22}(x,t;y,\tau)u(y,\tau)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}G_{22}(x,t;y,\tau)\left((p-p(1,1))-\frac{\mu u_{y}}{v}\right)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{G}_{23}(x-y,t)\left(E(y,0)-c_{v}\right)dy-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tau}\mathbb{G}_{23}(x-y,t-\tau)\left(E(y,\tau)-c_{v}\right)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{23}(x-y,t-\tau)\left((pu)-\left(\frac{\kappa}{v}\theta_{y}+\frac{\mu}{v}uu_{y}\right)\right)dyd\tau.

In above formula, we obtain
u(x,t)
since
G_{22}(x-y;0)=\delta(x-y)
, and we do not have other boundary term at
t
because the Green’s function
\mathbb{G}(x,0)=I
and thus
\mathbb{G}_{ij}(x,0)=0
when
i\neq j
. Then, we immediately obtain a representation of
u(x,t)
as follows,

(4.7)

\displaystyle u(x,t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{G}_{21}(x-y,t)(v(y,0)-1)dy+\int_{\mathbb{R}}G_{22}(x,t;y,0)u(y,0)dy+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{G}_{23}(x-y,t)\left(E(y,0)-c_{v}\right)dy

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tau}\mathbb{G}_{21}(x-y,t-\tau)(v(y,\tau)-1)dyd\tau-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{21}(x-y,t-\tau)u(y,\tau)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tau}G_{22}(x,t;y,\tau)u(y,\tau)dyd\tau+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}G_{22}(x,t;y,\tau)\left(p-p(1,1)-\frac{\mu u_{y}}{v}\right)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tau}\mathbb{G}_{23}(x-y,t-\tau)\left(E(y,\tau)-c_{v}\right)dyd\tau+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{23}(x-y,t-\tau)\left((pu)-\left(\frac{\kappa}{v}\theta_{y}+\frac{\mu}{v}uu_{y}\right)\right)dyd\tau

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{9}\mathcal{I}_{i}.

Now, we apply (
4.3
) to represent
\mathbb{G}_{\tau}
in terms of derivative in
y
. Actually, for
\mathcal{I}_{4}
and
\mathcal{I}_{8}
in (
4.7
), we have the following representation,

(4.8)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{4}=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tau}\mathbb{G}_{21}(x-y,t-\tau)(v(y,\tau)-1)dyd\tau=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}K\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{22}(x-y,t-\tau)(v(y,\tau)-1)dyd\tau,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{8}=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tau}\mathbb{G}_{23}(x-y,t-\tau)\left(E(y,\tau)-c_{v}\right)dyd\tau

(4.9)

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt=-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\frac{K}{c_{v}}\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{22}(x-y,t-\tau)+\frac{\kappa}{c_{v}}\partial_{y}^{2}\mathbb{G}_{23}(x-y,t-\tau)\right)\left(E(y,\tau)-c_{v}\right)dyd\tau.

Moreover, according to (
4.5
), the effective Green’s function is constructed by partition of unit. Therefore, when dealing with
G_{22}
, we need to split the integral into three parts. More precisely, for
\mathcal{I}_{6}
in (
4.7
), we have the representation as below,

(4.10)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{6}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\tau}G_{22}(x,t;y,\tau)u(y,\tau)dyd\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t-2\nu_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{21}-K\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{23}-\mu\partial_{y}^{2}\mathbb{G}_{22}\right)(x-y,t-\tau)u(y,\tau)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{t-2\nu_{0}}^{t-\nu_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}-\frac{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\nu_{0}}\right)}{\nu_{0}}H\left(x,t;y,\tau;\frac{\mu}{v}\right)u(y,\tau)+\frac{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\nu_{0}}\right)}{\nu_{0}}\mathbb{G}_{22}(x-y;t-\tau)u(y,\tau)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{t-2\nu_{0}}^{t-\nu_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}-\mathcal{X}\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\nu_{0}}\right)\left(\frac{\mu}{v}H_{y}\left(x,t;y,\tau;\frac{\mu}{v}\right)\right)_{y}u(y,\tau)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{t-2\nu_{0}}^{t-\nu_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(1-\mathcal{X}\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\nu_{0}}\right)\right)\left(\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{21}-K\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{23}-\mu\partial_{y}^{2}\mathbb{G}_{22}\right)(x-y,t-\tau)u(y,\tau)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{t-\nu_{0}}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}-\left(\frac{\mu}{v}H_{y}\left(x,t;y,\tau;\frac{\mu}{v}\right)\right)_{y}u(y,\tau)dyd\tau.

Similarly, for
\mathcal{I}_{7}
in (
4.7
), we have the following representation,

(4.11)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{7}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}G_{22}(x,t;y,\tau)\left(p-p(1,1)-\frac{\mu u_{y}}{v}\right)dyd\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t-2\nu_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{22}(x,t;y,\tau)\left(p-p(1,1)-\frac{\mu u_{y}}{v}\right)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{t-2\nu_{0}}^{t-\nu_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{X}\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\nu_{0}}\right)H_{y}\left(x,t;y,\tau;\frac{\mu}{v}\right)\left(p-p(1,1)-\frac{\mu u_{y}}{v}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{t-2\nu_{0}}^{t-\nu_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(1-\mathcal{X}\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\nu_{0}}\right)\right)\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{22}(x-y;t-\tau)\left(p-p(1,1)-\frac{\mu u_{y}}{v}\right)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{t-\nu_{0}}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}H_{y}\left(x,t;y,\tau;\frac{\mu}{v}\right)\left(p-p(1,1)-\frac{\mu u_{y}}{v}\right).

Now, we combine the representations in (
4.7
), (
4.8
), (
4.9
), (
4.10
) and (
4.11
), and apply the backward equation (
4.3
) to obtain a new representation of
u(x,t)
as follows,

(4.12)

\displaystyle u(x,t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{G}_{21}(x-y,t)(v(y,0)-1)dy+\int_{\mathbb{R}}G_{22}(x,t;y,0)u(y,0)dy+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{G}_{23}(x-y,t)\left(E(y,0)-c_{v}\right)dy

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}K\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{22}(x-y,t-\tau)(v(y,\tau)-1)dyd\tau-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{21}(x-y,t-\tau)u(y,\tau)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{0}^{t-2\nu_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{21}-K\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{23}-\mu\partial_{y}^{2}\mathbb{G}_{22}\right)(x-y,t-\tau)u(y,\tau)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{t-2\nu_{0}}^{t-\nu_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}-\frac{1}{\nu_{0}}\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\nu_{0}}\right)H\left(x,t;y,\tau;\frac{\mu}{v}\right)u(y,\tau)+\frac{1}{\nu_{0}}\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\nu_{0}}\right)\mathbb{G}_{22}(x-y;t-\tau)u(y,\tau)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{t-2\nu_{0}}^{t-\nu_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}-\mathcal{X}\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\nu_{0}}\right)\left(\frac{\mu}{v}H_{y}\left(x,t;y,\tau;\frac{\mu}{v}\right)\right)_{y}u(y,\tau)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{t-2\nu_{0}}^{t-\nu_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(1-\mathcal{X}\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\nu_{0}}\right)\right)\left(\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{21}-K\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{23}-\mu\partial_{y}^{2}\mathbb{G}_{22}\right)(x-y,t-\tau)u(y,\tau)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{t-\nu_{0}}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}-\left(\frac{\mu}{v}H_{y}\left(x,t;y,\tau;\frac{\mu}{v}\right)\right)_{y}u(y,\tau)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{0}^{t-2\nu_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{y}\mathbb{G}_{22}(x,t;y,\tau)\left(p-p(1,1)-\frac{\mu u_{y}}{v}\right)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\int_{t-2\nu_{0}}^{t-\nu_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{X}\left(\frac{t-\tau}{\nu_{0}}\right)H_{y}\left(x,t;y,\tau;\frac{\mu}{v}\right)\left(p-p(1,1)-\frac{\mu u_{y}}{v}\right)dyd\tau
